description paragraph 3697   1028571:1029052  Description
Frb clitocyboid, not hygrophanous. Cap 15-240(-400) mm, depressed to funnel-shaped, velvety to finely scaly, white to pale buff to buff, pinkish buff, yellowish, yellowish brown, orange brown, reddish brown or greyish brown. Gills decurrent to deeply decurrent. Smell faint, ± cyanic or sweetaromatic. Sp deposit whitish. Sp ± dacryoid, smooth, hyaline, without iodine reactions. Basidia 4-spored. Cheilocystidia absent. Pileipellis a trichoderm. Clamps present.
▸ gap before description@3701 — 1 block(s): Misc-exposition

    Saprotrophic on soil or litter in forests, grassland and alpine habitats. 
10 species in the Nordic countries, 11 species in the key. 
The genus used to be a part of Clitocybe, but molecular results have shown that they are not 
closely related.

Macrocystidiaceae Kuhner
description paragraph 3723   1036855:1037648  Description
Frb agaricoid, hymenophore gilled. Cap 10-50 mm, convex to bell-shaped, smooth, dry, greasy, dark brown to orange brown. Stem normally developed, fleshy. Veil absent. Smell of cut cucumber or marinated herring. Sp deposit brownish pink. Sp ellipsoid, hyaline, rather thick-walled with a complex spore wall with several layers, without germ pore, smooth, non-amyloid, cyanophilous, with hilum of unknown type, uninucleate. Basidia without siderophilous granulation. Cystidia present as cheilocystidia, pleurocystidia, pileocystidia and caulocystidia, thin-walled, smooth. Hymenophoral trama regular to irregular. Pileipellis a cutis. Clamps present. Saprotrophic on the ground on rich soil in forests and scrubs. Anamorph state present; rhexolytic secession of conidia.

Pleurotaceae Kuhner
description paragraph 3737   1039270:1040488  Description
Frb agaricoid, hymenophore gilled. Cap 2-200 mm, convex, shell-shaped, applanate, funnel-shaped, smooth or finely scaly, dry to greasy, whitish, grey, brown to pale blue; stem reduced or absent. Veil present or absent; ring-like structure present or absent; trama with a gelatinous layer in Hohenbuehclia. Smell farinaceous or indistinct. Sp deposit white, cream, pink or pale greyish lilaceous. Sp cylindrical, allantoid, subglobose or ellipsoid, hyaline, thin-walled, without germ pore, smooth, non-amyloid, not cyanophilous, with hilum of the nodulose type. Sp uninucleate. Basidia without siderophilous granulation. Cystidia absent or present as thin-walled cheilocystidia and often also as thick-walled, apically incrusted metuloids. Hymenophoral trama irregular. Pileipellis a cutis or a trichoderm. Clamps present. Hyphal system monomitic or dimitic, if monomitic sometimes with some sclerified hyphae. Saprotrophic on trunks, stumps, branches, wood and dead herbs; in forests, scrubs and bogs. Anamorphic state present; producing blastoconidia in the Nematoctonus-anamorph of Hohcnbuehelia able to catch and use nematodes for food, whereas Pleurotus exudes toxic droplets for the same

biology paragraph 3759   1042527:1042848  Biology
Saprotrophic, solitary or in small groups, on dead wood of deciduous trees and conifers, rarely on grasses and mosses. The associated anamorph Nematoctonus Drechsler produces conidia and traps nematodes on glutinous cells similar to the capitulum of the cheilocystidia. 16 species in the Nordic countries.

    description paragraph 3861   1071880:1073038  Description
    Frb pleurotoid. Cap (10-)20-150(-350) mm wide, fan-, shell-, kidney-shaped or semicircular, convex to plane or umbilicate, dry to viscid, glabrous to scaly, sometimes tomentose at centre, white, pale brownish to bluish grey. Gills crowded,± decurrent, white to greyish. Stem absent or 10-110 x 7-35 mm, base often tomentose, whitish. Veil absent or present. Flesh tough, thick. Smell indistinct, farinaceous, fruity or like honey or aniseed. Taste mild, bitter or astringent, acidulous to farinaceous or fungoid. Sp deposit white, cream, yellowish or pale lilac. Sp 7-15 x 2.5-5.5 11m, cylindrical, smooth, thin-walled, hyaline, without iodine reactions, not cyanophilous. Basidia 4-spored, rarely 2-spored. Cystidia absent, but sometimes with lecythiform cystidia-like basidioles. Hyphal system monomitic or dimitic with skeletal hyphae. Gill trama irregular. Pileipellis a cutis. Clamps present. Saprotrophic or weakly parasitic, solitary or imbricate in groups, on living and dead deciduous wood, more rarely on conifers, producing a white rot. Lit.: Boekhout in Bas et al. 1990, Hilber 1985, Pilat 1935, Watling & Gregory 1989.

      Saprotrophic on trunks, stumps and branches of deciduous and rarely coniferous wood in forests and scrubs. 
Anamorphic state absent. 
The phylogenetic position of this genus is unresolved, and seemingly it forms its own clade. 
The former anticipated close relationship to Plrnrotaceae, especially Hohenbuehelia, has not been 
confirmed by molecular analyses (Moncalvo et al. 2002, Thorn et al. 2005).

    Frb agaricoid with gilled hymenophore. T. decora (Fr.: Fr.) Singer 
Pluteaceae Kotl. & Pouzar 
Frb agaricoid, hymenophore gilled. Cap 10-210 mm, convex, applanate, hemispherical, smooth 
or hairy-scaly, dry, greasy or viscid, brown, grey, yellow, white or red. Gills free, pink at maturity; 
stem normally developed, fleshy. Veil present as a volva or absent. Smell indistinct. Sp deposit pink 
or pinkish brown. 
Sp subglobose to ellipsoid, slightly yellowish to pale brownish, moderately thick-walled, without germ pore, smooth, non-amyloid, cyanophilous, with hilum of the open pore type, in most species uninucleate, in some binucleate. Basidia without siderophilous granulation. Cystidia absent 
or more often present as cheilocystidia or pleurocystidia, thin- or thick-walled, smooth or apically 
incrusted or with characteristic apical hooks. Hymenophoral trama inverse. Pileipellis a cutis or 
hymeniderm. Clamps present or absent. Hyphae in stem multinucleate.

    description paragraph 4007   1124422:1125378  Description
    Frb with free, pink gills. Cap 15-150 mm, convex to plane, often with an umbo, glabrous, felty or scaly, in some species venose, dry to slightly viscid, white, yellow, orange, brown or greyish, in some species hygrophanous and translucently striate. Gills free, white or whitish, becoming salmon, pink or pinkish brown with age, in some species with a dark brown edge. Stem smooth or minutely floccose. Smell raphanoid, sligthly sweetish or indistinct. Taste indistinct to raphanoid, often somewhat nauseous. Sp deposit pink to pinkish brown. Clamps absent, more rarely present.

    notes paragraph 4015   1125683:1126229  Notes
    sidered delicious, and no species known as poisonous. Several species groups are poorly resolved taxonomically, and especially P. plautus s.l. (incl. P. semibulbosus), P. phlebophorus s.l. and P. ephebeus are in need of a critical revision utilizing molecular techniques. Albino-forms and varieties have been described in several taxa, and it is possible that also the white-capped taxa, P. pellitus and P. inquilinus are albino forms of various species in their respective sections. Lit.: Orton 1986, Vellinga in Bas et al. 1990.

      Saprotrophic on the ground or decaying wood, exceptionally hothouses or in buildings, mostly 
in cultural environment, or parasitic on decaying frb of Clitocybe nebularis. Several species are considered inedible, but V volvacea is cultured and an economically important edible fungus.

Gills emarginate to narrowly or broadly adnate, rarely short decurrent, mostly crowded, white to grey or brownish or tinged salmon to orange. Stem smooth to fibrillose, often pruinose, in one species punctate-finely scaly. Veil absent. Smell mostly faint, less often aniseed-like or sweetish-aromatical. Taste mild to acrid and unpleasant. Sp deposit white to pale yellowish. Sp ellipsoid, hyaline, with amyloid warts. Basidia mostly 4-spored. Cheilocystidia mostly present, either ± urticoid and mostly septate and thin-walled (figs 349B-C) or fusiform to lageniforrn and mostly aseptate and slightly thick-walled (figs 349D-G), sometimes with crystals at apex. Pleurocystidia present or absent, similar to cheilocystidia. Pileipellis a cutis, an ixocutis or a trichoderm. Clamps absent.

Favolaschiaceae Singer 
Panellaceae Julich 
Frb agaricoid or boletoid, hymenophore gilled or poroid. Cap 1-50 mm, convex, applanate, conical, 
bell-shaped or shell-shaped, smooth, dry to viscid, often hygrophanously striate, white, grey or 
brown, rarely yellow, orange, red, violaceous, lilaceous, greenish or bluish. Gills adnate, adnexed, 
emarginate or decurrent. Stem fleshy, normally developed, or reduced or absent. Veil absent or 
rarely present. Flesh in cap sometimes with a gelatinous layer. Smell indistinct, farinaceous, nitrous 
or sometimes aromatic or of iodoform. Sp deposit white. 
Sp ellipsoid, subglobose or allantoid, thin-walled, without germ pore, smooth, generally amyloid but in Mycena also non-amyloid, not cyanophilous, with hilum of the nodulose type, uninucleate. Basidia without siderophilous granulation. Cystidia present as cheilocystidia or pleurocystidia, thin-walled, smooth or with diverticulae. Hymenophoral trama regular. Pileipellis a cutis or a 
hymeniderm. Clamps present or absent; trama of the stem and of gills dextrinoid or not. 
Saprotrophic on soil, debris, trunks, stumps, branches, wood, herbs; in all types of localities. 
Anamorphic state absent.

      ▸ gap before description@4175 — 1 block(s): Misc-exposition; 1 furniture hidden

        FAVOLASCHIACEAE: MYCENA 
353
      description paragraph 4175   1185231:1186765  Description
      in dull grey brown shades, some yellow, red, blue, purple or black. Gills almost free or pseudocollarioid to deeply decurrent, distant to crowded, but mostly medium spaced, sometimes with strong interveining, variously coloured but often pale, some with contrasting edge colour. Stem typically cylindrical and rather thin, fragile to very tough, smooth, pruinose, floccose to hairy, base often with strigose hairs but may be smooth and inserted or with distinct downy disc. Smell often diagnostic, iodoform, radish and nitrous smells are common, when not mentioned the smell is indistinct. Taste insignificant, mealy or bitter. Sp deposit white. Sp globose to cylindrical, often dacryoid, smooth, hyaline, thin-walled, amyloid, more rarely without iodine reactions. Basidia 2-4-spored, ± clavate, when not mentioned the basidia are 4-spored. Cheilo- and often pleurocystidia present and diagnostic or very rarely absent, either smooth or with variously shaped short and regular to highly irregular and long diverticulae over entire length or on top or only in median part. Pileocystidia or terminal elements of pileipellis elements also diagnostic and often with diverticulae, which may be so numerous as to appear coralloid. Caulocystidia or terminal elements of stipitipellis hyphae likewise important. Gill trama mostly dextrinoid (vinaceous) but not reacting in some groups, and this typically coinciding with non-amyloidity of spores. Clamps present or absent, when not mentioned clamps are present.
      biology paragraph 4177   1186767:1187371  Biology
      Saprotrophic, outside the area also parasitic; on litter of all kinds, fern rhizomes, herbs, all kinds of dead wood, decaying fruits, etc., also on humose soil, including burnt turf. 99 species in the Nordic countries, 104 species in the key. M. alphithophora is not included since it only occurs in indoor environments in the Nordic countries. At least M. rosea is known to be poisonous, but other members of sect. Calodo11tes are equally suspect. Species of Mycena are often attacked by the zygomycetous parasite Spi11ellus fusiger. For further information and a wealth of pictures see

described; it has also been suggested (Arnolds 1982) that it is conspecific with M. citrinomarginata, 
which name would have precedence. Molecular studies are awaited to resolve these questions.

      description paragraph 4553   1310782:1312407  Description
      Frb generally fragile; stem very pale to silvery white. Cap 15-60 mm, conical to bell-shaped, becoming almost flat, pale mouse grey to off white, the disc lightly coloured with vinaceous or clay buff or drab; gills ventricose, narrowly adnate L = 22-28(-30), white; stem (25-)3580(-100) mm, sometimes with shallow longitudinal grooves, pale grey to shiny silky white, sometimes with a blue tone; smell usually nitrous. Sp (7-)8-10(-12) x (5-)5.5-7(-8) µm,, Qav = c. 1.6, ellipsoid, amyloid; cheilocystidia, fusiform, with the tip often mucronate or beaked (fig. 385D); pleurocystidia similar; caulocystidia up to 10 µm wide, attenuated to a narrow tip, these may be curved to the shape of a walking stick handle. Scattered or in small fascicles, on wood from broadleaved trees, often in rich calcareous habitats; early summer to autumn; occasional in temp.-bore.; DK (DD), FI (Le), NO (Le), SE (Le). - R&H 363, Rob 275. M. niveipes (Murrill) Murrill Frb less fragile, with darker colours, grey brown to dark sepia. Cap 10-30 mm, paraboloid, becoming convex to bell-shaped, hygrophanous, ± sulcate, blackish brown at the centre, shading through sepia to greyish brown; gills ventricose and adnate, L = 20-26, pale grey; stem 30-90 x 1-3 mm, blackish grey with a brownish cast, paler to whitish at the top, sometimes becoming entirely white on drying; usually with a nitrous odour. Sp 7-10 x 4.5-7 µm, Q = c. 1.6, ellipsoid, amyloid; cheilocystidia variable, clavate, sometimes with digital extensions or like the abundant pleurocystidia fusiform like those of M. plumipes, fig. 377D. - M&B 2:12.

        M. algeriensis Maire 
Note: the Nordic occurrence of this poorly understood taxon, should be re-evaluated but is currently 
listed from DK (oo), FI (oo), NO (NE), SE (Le). This description is based on a collection from Finnish Lapland and Kuhner's description. The collection did not reveal any clamps in spite of a careful search. 
Maas Geesteranus suggests that it should have clamps, Kuhner does not mention any. It is included in 
the key to raise awareness.

Frb agaricoid with gilled hymenophore. Cap 20-200 mm, convex, applanate, hemispherical, smooth
or covered with loose scales or membranous fragments, dry, greasy or viscid, white, grey, yellow,
orange, red, brown, olivaceous green. Gills free. Stem normally developed, fleshy. Veil present or
absent; ring present or absent; volva present sometimes in the form of scale-belts. Smell indistinct
or sweetish. Sp deposit white.
Sp subglobose to ellipsoid, hyaline, thin-walled, without germ pore, smooth, amyloid or nonamyloid, not cyanophilous, with hilum of the nodulose type, binucleate. Basidia without siderophilous granulation. Cystidia absent, but spheropedunculate cells present on the gill edge of Amanita,
resembling cystidia. Hymenophoral trama first divergent or regular, becoming irregular. Pileipellis
a cutis or ixocutis. Clamps present or absent.
Mycorrhizal with deciduous and coniferous trees and scrubs or saprotrophic on the ground.
Anamorphic state absent.
Note: The family also includes the sequestrate genus Torrendia Bres.

Frb with central stem, free gills, volva and often ring. Cap 20-200 mm, hemispherical, conical, ovoid
or convex, later expanding with or without an umbo, smooth, radially sulcate at margin or not,

or grey. Gills free, white or whitish, rarely pale greyish buff or greenish. Stem fibrillose to floccose,
sometimes with girdles, white or tinged like cap. Universal veil whitish, yellowish or greyish, in
some species staining brownish, in some species membranous forming a volva at the stem base,
in other species breaking up into warts or patches on the cap. Partial veil absent or membranous,
whitish, striate above or not, forming a ring on the stem. Smell mostly indistinct, but in some species nauseous honey-like or like raw potato. Sp deposit white.

4-spored. Cheilocystidia absent or little differentiated. Pileipellis a cutis or an ixocutis. Clamps
present or absent.
Ectomycorrhizal, in association with deciduous and coniferous hosts. Summer to autumn. Several species poisonous, some even deadly.

sidered delicious, and no species known as poisonous. Several species groups are poorly resolved
taxonomically, and especially P. plautus s.l. (incl. P. semibulbosus), P. phlebophorus s.l. and P. ephebeus

inquilinus are albino forms of various species in their respective sections.

not easy to separate, there being no microscopical characters yet identified to separate them. For this
reason they are not included in this key. We have found apparently different varieties adjacent to each
other possibly on the same mycelium, with no good characters to separate them. We have just included
the two varieties with 2-spored basidia.

      biology (4198 chars)

      Saprotrophic, solitary or in small groups, on dead wood of deciduous trees and conifers, rarely
on grasses and mosses. The associated anamorph Nematoctonus Drechsler produces conidia and
traps nematodes on glutinous cells similar to the capitulum of the cheilocystidia.
16 species in the Nordic countries.

T. decora (Fr.: Fr.) Singer
Pluteaceae Kotl. & Pouzar
Frb agaricoid, hymenophore gilled. Cap 10-210 mm, convex, applanate, hemispherical, smooth
or hairy-scaly, dry, greasy or viscid, brown, grey, yellow, white or red. Gills free, pink at maturity;
stem normally developed, fleshy. Veil present as a volva or absent. Smell indistinct. Sp deposit pink
or pinkish brown.
Sp subglobose to ellipsoid, slightly yellowish to pale brownish, moderately thick-walled, without germ pore, smooth, non-amyloid, cyanophilous, with hilum of the open pore type, in most species uninucleate, in some binucleate. Basidia without siderophilous granulation. Cystidia absent
or more often present as cheilocystidia or pleurocystidia, thin- or thick-walled, smooth or apically
incrusted or with characteristic apical hooks. Hymenophoral trama inverse. Pileipellis a cutis or
hymeniderm. Clamps present or absent. Hyphae in stem multinucleate.

Favolaschiaceae Singer
Panellaceae Julich
Frb agaricoid or boletoid, hymenophore gilled or poroid. Cap 1-50 mm, convex, applanate, conical,
bell-shaped or shell-shaped, smooth, dry to viscid, often hygrophanously striate, white, grey or
brown, rarely yellow, orange, red, violaceous, lilaceous, greenish or bluish. Gills adnate, adnexed,
emarginate or decurrent. Stem fleshy, normally developed, or reduced or absent. Veil absent or
rarely present. Flesh in cap sometimes with a gelatinous layer. Smell indistinct, farinaceous, nitrous
or sometimes aromatic or of iodoform. Sp deposit white.
Sp ellipsoid, subglobose or allantoid, thin-walled, without germ pore, smooth, generally amyloid but in Mycena also non-amyloid, not cyanophilous, with hilum of the nodulose type, uninucleate. Basidia without siderophilous granulation. Cystidia present as cheilocystidia or pleurocystidia, thin-walled, smooth or with diverticulae. Hymenophoral trama regular. Pileipellis a cutis or a
hymeniderm. Clamps present or absent; trama of the stem and of gills dextrinoid or not.
Saprotrophic on soil, debris, trunks, stumps, branches, wood, herbs; in all types of localities.
Anamorphic state absent.

material; gill edge partly fertile, with tracts of basidia between cheilocystidia
see M. maculata 29
Frb without general reddish markings, although older material may show some
red or pink colouration; gill edge sterile, with continuous cheilocystidia
39
39.
Cap blackish grey to fuscous black; on roots of ericaceous shrubs or burnt roots
of Ulex, often radicating to the substrate; cheilocystidia clavate, heads often
branched (fig. 377C) (collections in this habitat were formerly referred to M.
uracea Pearson)
see M. megaspora key J 20
